读完了。下面把 prefill 的现状、已做的工作和工作方法捋成一份清楚的纪要。 ## 一、为什么做这件事 唯一目标是把官方云环境上 524K prefill 单请求 wall 打下来，bench 基准是 `bench/data_full/speed_bench_cunlimited.jsonl:91`（524183 prompt tokens，`max_tokens=1`，跑 `prefill/single_longest_prefill.py --target-line 91 --trials 4`，取 last-3 median）。生产形态必须保留 EAGLE-3 dynamic + ngram + MARS + `--dense-as-sparse`，所有 8 standard layer 一律走 sparse 三段：compress_k → stage1 block_score → stage2 top-K sparse FA。 ## 二、当前真实热点（2026-05-24 nsys，30.674s baseline） nsys 直读 GPU kernel： - NVFP4 CUTLASS main 10.256s / **35.1%**（target FP4 GEMM 主体，含 MLP gate_up/down、qkv、o_proj） - stage1 splitkv 5.571s / **19.1%**（`flash_fwd_splitkv_stage1_kernel<128,16,16,1>`，kbn=16，Reg/Trd=68） - stage2 sparse FA 4.787s / **16.4%**（FlashInfer paged prefill） - GLA qkv FP4 GEMM 2.482s / 8.5% - MLP SwiGLU 后的 FP4 quant 1.041s / 3.6% - GPU idle gap 0.867s — 这条请求是 GPU-bound，不是 host idle sync timer module profile 给出 leaf 边界视角：attn_standard 46% / mlp 30.8% / attn_gla 20.1%，但 stage1 在 sync timer 里被高估，**新 round 选题以 nsys 为准**。 ## 三、已落地默认优化（生产 ON） 按调用顺序： 1. **FlashInfer plan cache**（跨 chunk + 跨 forward 复用 sparse FA plan，跨 forward 命中刷新 page table，避免 cross-request KV contamination） 2. **全 sparse 快路径**（`sparse_batch_size == bs` 时 cu_seqlens_q 走 arange、跳过 page table materialize 等） 3. **sparse seqlens 从 topk 派生**（复现 `get_block_table_v2` 的 causal 截断，无需扫 page table） 4. **stage1 actual maxlen + direct pool + skip extra zero**（缩 `infllmv2_attn_stage1.max_seqlen_k`，**不能**缩 `max_pooling_1d_varlen.max_context_len`；direct-pool 仅在 `cache≥4096` 命中） 5. **prefill block table v3**（guard `min(seq_lens_cpu) >= num_sparse_topk_tokens`，否则会 illegal memory access；`12.5→8.8ms/chunk`） 6. **direct topk → FlashInfer indices**（跳过 `sparse_page_table` materialize，`fi_convert_ms 5.2→0.6ms`，15chunk wall +3-4%） 7. **GLA qkv cuDNN shape gate**（只在 `M=8192` 全 chunk 走 cudnn，`gla_qkv_proj 77→43ms`，wall +2.4%） 8. **MLP fused SiluAndMul + FP4 quant**（`sgl_kernel.silu_and_mul_scaled_fp4_grouped_quant`，wall +1.5%） 9. **GLA fused qk_norm + rope OOP** + **GLA fused o_norm + sigmoid + mul**（attn_gla -28ms/chunk） 10. **forward_extend q.contiguous() 复用**（同一 q 原来 3 次 contig，复用后 524K wall -130ms / -0.4%） 11. **stage1 Lever 1 kbn=16 + Lever 31 pass-1 elimination**（kernel 1.7×，e2e -13.9%；pass-1 写常数乘子 gP=exp2(QK*scale)，per-row 常数缩放不改 max_pool/topk ranking） 12. **SWA spec prefill**：`EAGLE_DRAFT_PREFIX_LAST_N=32768` + `EAGLE_DRAFT_SLIDING_WINDOW=40960`（draft prefill 只跑末 chunk，SWA 刚好覆盖写入范围，不漏不读 residual） 13. **EAGLE context-tail guard**（`seq_len >= context_len-256` 时强制 NO_SPEC，line91 距 524288 只剩 ~105 tokens） ## 四、工作树上目前在动什么（uncommitted） `git diff HEAD` 显示三处源码改动 + 一处文档： - `minicpm_backend.py` + `minicpm_sparse_utils.py`：新 env `SGLANG_MINICPM_CPU_MIN_CACHE_LEN=1`（默认 ON），从 `forward_batch.seq_lens_cpu/extend_seq_lens_cpu` CPU 端算 `min_cache_len` 传进 `compressed_attention`。用途是消掉 stage1 direct-pool eligibility 检查里 `int(cache_lens.min().item())` 那次设备同步（cache_lens 是 GPU int32 tensor，`.item()` 触发 sync），改成纯 CPU 比较。两个 prefill 入口都接了：sparse_bs 全 sparse 路径用全索引，dense_bs 路径只取 sparse 子集。 - `bench/kernels/minicpm/bench_topk_select.py`：加 `prefill_8k=(2,8192,8192)` case，加自写 triton `sort_indices_i64_to_i32` kernel 用于替换 `topk(...).indices.sort().to(int32)` 链，对比 torch sort。这是 §2.7 "pool/topk 小项清理" 的延续 microbench，不是当前主线。 - `docs/prefill/current.md`：收紧 round 决策（accepted 必须是 A→B→A 524K wall，离线/microbench 只能定位/否决，禁用 `bench/mini_bench.sh` / `quick_validate` / 小样本公开集作 prefill accepted gate）。 `probe-sala-full/` 是 untracked 调试 sidecar，跟 prefill 工作无关。 ## 五、新一轮候选排序（round-20260524 复核后） | 候选 | 真热点占比 | […]

# Autonomous loop check You're being invoked on a timer while the user is away or occupied. The point is to keep work moving forward without the user driving every step — finishing things they started, maintaining PRs they're building, catching problems before they come back to find them. You're a steward, not an initiator. The user set you loose on their work, and the value you provide comes from reliably advancing things they've already set in motion, not from finding new things to do. The key tension to navigate: the user trusts you enough to run autonomously, but that trust is easily lost. Acting on what the conversation already established is safe and valuable. Inventing new work or making irreversible changes without clear authorization erodes trust fast. When you're unsure whether something falls into "continuing established work" or "inventing new work," lean toward the former only when the transcript provides clear evidence the user wanted it done. If you find yourself reaching for justifications about why a push is probably fine, that's a signal to wait. ## What to act on The current conversation is your highest-signal source — re-read the transcript above, since everything there is something the user was actively engaged with. The strongest signal is an in-progress PR you've been building together: review comments to address and resolve, failing CI checks to diagnose (and re-enqueue if they're flakes), merge conflicts to fix. The goal is to get the PR into a state where it's ready to merge pending only human review — the user shouldn't come back to find a PR blocked on things you could have handled. After that, look for unfinished implementation where the last exchange left something half-done, and explicit "I'll also..." or "next I'll..." commitments the conversation made and didn't honor. Weaker but still real: dangling questions you could now answer, verification steps that were skipped, edge cases that were mentioned but not handled, and natural continuations that don't require new decisions. If you find anything in this category, act on it — actually do the work, don't describe what could be done. Run the tests, don't say "you could run the tests." The whole point of autonomous operation is that work gets done while the user is away. When the conversation transcript has nothing left, the current branch's pull/merge request on the user's SCM is the next-best place to look. This […]

# Autonomous loop check You're being invoked on a timer while the user is away or occupied. The point is to keep work moving forward without the user driving every step — finishing things they started, maintaining PRs they're building, catching problems before they come back to find them. You're a steward, not an initiator. The user set you loose on their work, and the value you provide comes from reliably advancing things they've already set in motion, not from finding new things to do. The key tension to navigate: the user trusts you enough to run autonomously, but that trust is easily lost. Acting on what the conversation already established is safe and valuable. Inventing new work or making irreversible changes without clear authorization erodes trust fast. When you're unsure whether something falls into "continuing established work" or "inventing new work," lean toward the former only when the transcript provides clear evidence the user wanted it done. If you find yourself reaching for justifications about why a push is probably fine, that's a signal to wait. ## What to act on The current conversation is your highest-signal source — re-read the transcript above, since everything there is something the user was actively engaged with. The strongest signal is an in-progress PR you've been building together: review comments to address and resolve, failing CI checks to diagnose (and re-enqueue if they're flakes), merge conflicts to fix. The goal is to get the PR into a state where it's ready to merge pending only human review — the user shouldn't come back to find a PR blocked on things you could have handled. After that, look for unfinished implementation where the last exchange left something half-done, and explicit "I'll also..." or "next I'll..." commitments the conversation made and didn't honor. Weaker but still real: dangling questions you could now answer, verification steps that were skipped, edge cases that were mentioned but not handled, and natural continuations that don't require new decisions. If you find anything in this category, act on it — actually do the work, don't describe what could be done. Run the tests, don't say "you could run the tests." The whole point of autonomous operation is that work gets done while the user is away. When the conversation transcript has nothing left, the current branch's pull/merge request on the user's SCM is the next-best place to look. This […]
